Text:"KMed-31B Technical ReportWe present KMed-31B, medical large language model post-trained from an openweight model for on-premises deployment with limited hardware. Post-training model for diverse medical tasks remains difficult, because improving one kind of task can lower performance on another. We attribute this to differences between tasks in expected output and available feedback. Medical question answering and diagnosis prediction have reference answers, whereas clinical inquiry has no single correct response and is judged with rubrics. We therefore divide medical training tasks into four categories by these two properties, train one specialist per category with reward that its feedback supports, and integrate the specialists into single student with Multi-teacher On-policy Delta Distillation (MOPD2). For clinical inquiry, we build Synthetic Health Dialogue, simulated corpus of consultations with lay users and clinicians whose patient cases span common clinical presentations in primary care. Relative to its base model, KMed-31B raises HealthBench Hard from 22.95 to 41.87 and HealthBench Professional from 37.92 to 50.20 in non-thinking mode, and from 23.80 to 51.48 and from 41.97 to 59.18 in thinking mode, while its scores on four multiple-choice medical benchmarks are maintained or slightly improved. KMed-31B has the highest unadjusted HealthBench Hard score among the compared models, including proprietary ones, and scores higher on HealthBench Professional than every open-weight model we compare, including models with more than ten times as many total parameters. Applied to another base model, Qwen3.8-27B, the same training recipe also raises both HealthBench scores. We describe the training recipe, data construction, and settings in detail so that institutions requiring on-premises deployment can develop medical models from open-weight foundations.Frontier-level large language models have demonstrated strong performance on medical question answering and health conversations [1, 2]. In real clinical settings, however, services are often not allowed to send patient data outside the institution and must therefore run models on premises with limited hardware resources. This requirement has motivated efforts to develop relatively small open-weight medical models [3, 4]. Despite their small size, these models must still answer knowledge-intensive medical questions, reason about clinical cases, and handle consultations with lay users and clinicians. Recent open-weight models with around 30B total parameters match much larger models on individual medical benchmarks, but their strengths are uneven across multiple-choice medical questions and health conversations. Gemma-4-31B-IT is on par with GPT-5.6-sol [5] on MedXpertQA Text but falls far behind it on HealthBench Hard and HealthBench Professional, whereas Muse-Glimmer-30B [6] approaches Qwen3.5-397B [7] on both HealthBench benchmarks while its MedXpertQA Text score falls well below that of Gemma-4-31B-IT (Section 4.2). Improving one of these capabilities through post-training can come at the expense of the other. ConRub-Med reports that rubric-based reinforcement learning (RL) on Qwen3-32B raises HealthPreprint. Figure 1: Medical performance vs. price in thinking and non-thinking modes. Medical performance is the mean of the scores on HealthBench Hard, HealthBench Professional, MedXpertQA Text, JAMA Clinical Challenge, NEJM AI QA, and MedQA-USMLE. Blended prices use 3:1 input-to-output token ratio. We plot Gemma-4-31B-IT at $0.25 per million tokens and assign the same price to KMed-31B. For the other models, we use the list prices reported by Artificial Analysis [8] (first-party API prices when available, otherwise the median across providers). Frontier curves are interpolated between Pareto-optimal points, and the arrow shows the thinking-mode increase from Gemma-4-31BIT to KMed-31B. Bench Hard from 9.46 to 33.17 while lowering MedXpertQA Text from 22.41 to 17.55 [9]. In an Apertus-70B data ablation of Fully Open Meditron, adding synthetic clinical vignettes to the supervised fine-tuning (SFT) mixture improves open-ended clinical preference but lowers MedQA accuracy by 1.33 points [10]. We observe similar pattern when fine-tuning Gemma-4-31B-IT on consultation dialogues, which improves HealthBench performance but lowers MedXpertQA Text and JAMA Clinical Challenge accuracy even with medical multiple-choice examples mixed in (Section 5.1). The recurrence of this trade-off across base models and across both SFT and RL leads us to focus on how the tasks are posed and supervised rather than on particular model or training method. Medical question answering has reference answers against which response can be checked, whereas clinical inquiry covers open-ended requests from lay users and clinicians that have no single correct response. The quality of clinical response depends on several aspects at once, such as its accuracy, its completeness, and its appropriateness to the users context, and its evaluation therefore requires rubrics that specify criteria for each aspect. Medical question answering and clinical inquiry thus differ in both expected output and available feedback. In this sense, they constitute distinct domains, as mathematical reasoning and open-ended chat do in general-purpose post-training. Such domains tend to interfere when trained together in single run [11, 12], and each therefore calls for its own training procedure. Prior medical post-training has addressed such interference by refining the training data mixture or by integrating separately trained specialists [13, 14]; we make this distinction explicit and use it to decide which specialists to train and how to train each one. We develop KMed-31B from Gemma-4-31B-IT through post-training recipe that groups medical tasks by expected output and available training feedback and assigns one specialist to each category (Section 2.1). Open-ended question answering, multiple-choice question answering, and diagnosis prediction provide reference answers, so their specialists are trained through RL with answer-matching rewards. In both modes, KMed-31B has the highest HealthBench Hard score among the models shown and"

To bridge this gap, we present NightWatch, plug-and-play framework that endows any CUA with continuous, temporally grounded perception without changing the agents model, prompt, or action space. NightWatch couples the VLM agent with NightWatch-0.8B, lightweight System-1 model that performs streaming monitoring of the interface state and keeps the agent synchronized with the evolving environment at low computational cost and latency, thereby grounding each decision in the current state rather than an outdated snapshot and reducing both stale actions and idle waiting while preserving task success. Forward diffusion reinforcement learning (RL)enabling deterministic ODE sampling without trajectory backpropagationhas grown increasingly popular for efficiently aligning diffusion models. However, we reveal that existing methods essentially rely on signed reward-weighted regression (RWR), regressing the trainable policy velocity onto rollouts from behavior distribution. Unbiased baseline subtraction implicitly assumes that the policy velocity equals the behavior marginal velocityan idealized assumption violated under classifier-free guidance (CFG) rollouts, offline/off-policy updates, and imperfect online self-consistency. This behaviorpolicy mismatch breaks baseline invariance and injects baselineinduced gradient drift, destabilizing training. To resolve this issue, we propose Instantaneous Trajectory Distribution Matching (iTDM), taking the infinitesimal limit of Trajectory Distribution Matching toward time-local reward-guided target via the posterior-score identity to yield sample-level gradient anchored at the behavior velocity without learning an additional reward-tilted target score. Text:"LoopedWAM: Looped World Action Models for Elastic Inference Anh Dao #World action models (WAMs) couple generative video modeling with robotic action prediction but typically require large video and action transformers, limiting deployment efficiency. We present LoopedWAM, compact WAM that recurrently applies shared six-block core in each expert, executing 30 transformer layers while storing only 12 blocks per expert. To account for their asymmetric inference costs, the video and action experts can operate at different depths: visual features are computed once per query, whereas action computation repeats at every sampling step. We further propose elastic self-distillation, which trains single elastic checkpoint, LoopedWAM-E, across action-depth and sampling-step budgets. With only 0.58B policy parameters and no robot pretraining, LoopedWAM achieves 96.1% success on LIBERO, 91.9% on RoboTwin 2.0, and 52.2% on RoboCasaGR1, while using 7.4 less peak GPU memory than Fast-WAM under matched inference measurements. On LIBERO, LoopedWAM-E achieves 96.0% success in 27.6 ms or 95.2% in 24.7 ms on an H100, providing multiple accuracylatency operating points from single checkpoint; with two sampling steps and four action loops, it stays within 0.5 points of LoopedWAM on all three benchmarks. Ablations demonstrate that weight-shared depth preserves competitive performance, while aggressive action-depth reduction requires explicit budget-aware training. On real AgileX PiPER arm, LoopedWAM succeeds in 71.3% of 80 trials across four tasks. (cid:128) Project page Code (cid:243) Models # Correspondence anh.dao@ucf.edu https://anhdao69.github.io/LoopedWAM https://github.com/anhdao69/LoopedWAM https://huggingface.co/anhdao69/LoopedWAM Figure 1. LoopedWAM is small, fast, and elastic. (a) Each expert stores 12 blocks and loops its shared 6-block core times. One checkpoint thus reaches the 30-layer depth of its dense donor with 2.4 fewer parameters, and the depth is chosen at test time. (b) RoboTwin 2.0 success against parameters, as reported by each paper. At 0.58B, LoopedWAM (91.9%) is the best model below 2B and on par with the 6B Fast-WAM (91.8%). (c) LIBERO success against latency on one H100 for each setting of action loops Ka and sampling steps of LoopedWAM-E. One checkpoint spans 24.786.2 ms at 95.296.2% success, and at (2, 2) it is 3.9 faster than Fast-WAM. 1 LoopedWAM: Looped World Action Models for Elastic Inference Dao et al.A practical robot policy must satisfy two competing demands: producing accurate actions from visual observations and language instructions, while operating within the GPU memory and latency constraints of closed-loop control. Recent world action models (WAMs) have made remarkable progress on the former by leveraging pretrained video diffusion models to learn robotic actions, often through jointly training video and action experts [5, 29, 33, 60, 63]. Drawing on physical priors from web-scale video pretraining, WAMs have achieved competitive performance with vision-language-action models (VLAs) on benchmarks such as LIBERO [39], RoboTwin 2.0 [12], and RoboCasa [45], often without robot pretraining. However, these gains come at cost that conflicts with the latter demand. Most current WAMs carry 2B to 14B parameters and repeatedly execute deep action expert across multiple denoising steps, whereas closed-loop control calls for small memory footprint, low latency, and the flexibility to trade accuracy for speed without retraining. Two natural approaches address different aspects of this challenge, but neither is sufficient on its own. Sharing weights across depth reduces the number of stored parameters without reducing the sequential computation required at fixed execution depth. Conversely, reducing the number of sampling steps lowers latency but may degrade action quality when the policy is not trained for fewer steps. practical compact WAM must therefore answer two questions: how much capacity must it store, and how much computation must it execute to produce useful actions? Answering these questions starts from where WAM spends its computation. Fast-WAM [63] showed that WAM need not generate future video at test time: the benefit of video modeling comes from joint training, allowing the video expert to encode the current observation once while the action expert generates an action chunk over sampling steps. query therefore requires one video-expert pass and action-expert passes. At batch size one, for comparable block architectures, latency is primarily determined by the number of sequentially executed transformer blocks rather than parameter count alone (Section 4.3, Figure 3c). These observations suggest two complementary opportunities: reducing parameter storage through weight sharing and reducing execution cost by allocating computation differently between the two experts. ① For the first question, we turn to looped, or weight-shared, transformers [2, 15, 20, 22], which reach the depth of deep network with the parameters of shallow one by repeatedly applying shared group of blocks. We bring this idea to world action models with LoopedWAM (Figure 1a). Each expert stores 12 blocks organized into 3-block prelude, 6-block shared core, and 3-block coda. Repeating the shared core four times recovers the 30-layer depth of its Wan2.1 donor [53]. The resulting policy has 0.58B parameters, 2.4 fewer than an equally deep dense model built from the same donor. Importantly, this makes execution depth potential test-time choice without changing the stored parameters. ② For the second question, the asymmetric inference costs of the two experts suggest that their execution depths can be controlled independently. Given this asymmetry, LoopedWAM allows the two experts to use different loop counts, with late alignment enabling even single action loop to access the deepest visual features. However, adjustable execution depth does not guarantee reliable inference at reduced depth: without elastic training, reducing the action expert to single loop drops LIBERO success to 12.3% under our ablation training schedule (Figure 3a). To address this, we introduce elastic self-distillation, which trains single checkpoint, LoopedWAM-E, to operate across action-loop counts Ka {1, 2, 4} and sampling steps {2, 5, 10}, while keeping the video expert at full depth. By matching frozen full-depth teacher starting from the students own intermediate states, the student learns to correct errors introduce"

Text:"November 24, 2026 Qwen-Image-HD: Autoregressive Diffusion for High Definition Image Generation Zihao Liu, Zekai Zhang, Shengming Yin, Zikai Zhou, Donghao Zhou, Chenyang Zhu, Lihan Jiang, Ningyuan Tang, Bingxuan Zhao, Yanran Zhang, Yan Shu, Zhening Liu, Jiahao Li, Jie Zhang, Kaiyuan Gao, Kun Yan, Qihang Ai, Rui Song, Shuo Cao, Xiao Xu, Xiaoyue Chen, Yuxiang Chen, Zhendong Wang, Zhouyang Li, Bo Zheng, Jun Zhou, Xiu Li, Chenfei WuFine-tuning generator on complete high-resolution canvas requires long image sequences and substantial computation, yet local regions can still exhibit unnatural repetitive stripes when training resources are limited. We introduce Qwen-Image-HD, which adapts Qwen-Image 2.0 to high-resolution synthesis through local training and blockwise autoregressive generation. Each prediction covers target tile at 1K scale, conditioned on high-resolution upper and left neighbors and compressed historical thumbnail. These conditions provide adjacent fine-scale structures and an overview of the scene generated so far. To integrate these complementary cues, we map both forms of history and the target to shared canvas coordinate system to express their spatial relationships and scale differences. On Qwen-Image-Bench at 4K, Qwen-ImageHD achieves stronger overall evaluation performance than direct 4K generation with FLUX-3-Image-Klein. Compared with full-image 4K supervised fine-tuning of the same backbone, it achieves better generation quality with lower training compute and higher inference efficiency, while exhibiting fewer unnatural repetitive stripes in originalresolution comparisons. We further introduce Wavefront-AutoRegressive inference schedule that generates tiles on the same anti-diagonal concurrently across GPUs to reduce single-image latency. Figure 1: Images generated by Qwen-Image-HD. Left: multiple 4K examples. Right: single 16K image with enlarged details. Corresponding author.High-definition text-to-image generation requires coherent global structure and fine local detail. Recent work (Zhang et al., 2025a; Ye et al., 2026) improves 4K synthesis by adapting pretrained generators through post-training with specialized data, representations, and objectives. However, training on complete high-resolution images requires processing long image sequences at every update. As the canvas grows, this increases the cost of adapting the generator, limiting the number of updates possible within given budget. Coherent composition alone also does not ensure that individual regions contain convincing detail. This motivates central question: how can pretrained generator acquire high-definition synthesis capabilities efficiently while preserving both scene structure and local detail? Blockwise prediction shortens the target sequence, but local targets still need context to form coherent image. NUWA-Infinity (Liang et al., 2022) combines patchand token-level autoregression, propagating historical information through cached neighborhood features. ACDiT (Hu et al., 2024) combines blockwise diffusion with skip-causal attention and KV caching, while MADFormer (Chen et al., 2025a) separates autoregressive conditioning from iterative diffusion refinement across model layers. Although both reuse historical computations, their retained context grows with the preceding sequence. Reducing target size therefore does not by itself bound the cost of conditioning on developing canvas. We seek history representation that supplies nearby detail and an explicit broader overview without retaining every preceding high-resolution token. We introduce Qwen-Image-HD, which adapts Qwen-Image 2.0 (Zhao et al., 2026) through 1K tile prediction with bounded, spatially organized history to generate high-definition images (Figure 1). fixed-size historical thumbnail summarizes completed canvas regions, while boundary regions of the upper and left neighbors retain fine structures lost during downsampling. Unfilled regions remain white, so the thumbnail represents only available history during both training and generation. Its token count stays fixed as more canvas regions are completed, providing an expanding scene overview without accumulating high-resolution history. These conditions serve complementary roles: the thumbnail conveys broader scene organization, and the neighbors constrain continuity with adjacent regions. Their different resolutions and spatial extents, however, require the model to distinguish where each condition belongs. We therefore map the thumbnail, neighbors, and target to shared canvas coordinates that preserve their relative positions and scales. This dual-scale representation connects local training targets to the larger scene within bounded context-token budget. At inference, Serial-AutoRegressive sampling uses the same context structure to assemble local predictions into complete image, updating history after each tile. Sequential sampling, however, increases single-image waiting time even when each prediction is inexpensive. We introduce Wavefront-AutoRegressive inference to generate tiles on the same anti-diagonal concurrently while preserving upper/left dependencies. Each round uses shared history, which is updated when the round completes. Our implementation combines concurrent tile generation with within-tile parallelism and dynamic GPU allocation to accelerate rounds with fewer active tiles. The schedule applies directly to the Serial-trained model without additional training, reducing latency through parallel execution with latencyquality trade-off relative to Serial. Paleographers call this diplomatic transcription. Vision-language models (VLMs) read handwriting well, but they also tend to rewrite it into correct language. We measure this behaviour on BRESSAY, dataset of 1,000 handwritten Brazilian Portuguese essays, with benchmark that adds error preservation, invented errors, edit-markup precision and recall and line structure to the character error rate (CER), and that compares systems with paired bootstrap confidence intervals. Because schools need models they can run on their own hardware, we restrict the study to open-weight systems. We evaluate 22 open baselines: 16 general-purpose VLMs from 2B to 400B parameters and five OCR-specialised VLMs, all without task-specific training, and recogniser trained on BRESSAY lines. The best of them, Qwen3-VL 235B-A22B, reaches 5.6% CER on the 199 test pages. None reproduces the edit markup reliably, and every VLM replaces 3978% of the words that are absent from Portuguese dictionary with dictionary words, including general VLMs whose prompt explicitly asks them not to. We then fine-tune LightOnOCR-3-1B on the 647 training pages in 85 minutes on one GPU. The resulting model, Escriba-OCR-1B, reaches 2.06% CER, 3.5 points below the best zero-shot baseline (95% CI [4.2, 2.9]), with 1.63% CER on the text alone. Under the case-sensitive metric of the ICDAR 2024 BRESSAY competition it reaches 2.1% CER, below the best published page-level results (2.22.4%). Abstract  Coding agents can construct and revise robot behavior during execution, but using one agent for the whole robot can delay otherwise independent work. We introduce Asynchronous Coding Agents as Policy (ACAP), an asynchronous framework that organizes one robot as a team of persistent agents. Each member reasons and acts for a configurable control group, such as an arm with its gripper or the torso and chassis, enabling asynchronous reasoning and execution. However, different robot components often have physical dependencies that require coordination. To address this challenge, ACAP introduces a hyper-agent that assigns tasks, coordinates dependencies, and dynamically allocates control between the lead and member agents. This allows independent work to progress concurrently while supporting tightly coupled manipulation when necessary. Across 72 real-world rollouts covering 25 task/platform settings, ACAP reduces task-balanced time cost by 28.2% compared with a single-agent Direct Policy, with improvements across Fully Parallel, Parallel-Sequential, and Collaborative tasks. Across all eight Collaborative settings, ACAP also achieves lower mean time cost than Fixed Delegation, where only member agents can control their assigned groups. An additional 90-rollout study shows lower mean 30-minute-capped time cost than Direct on all six tasks. We further demonstrate mobile manipulation using the same member interface for chassis and torso control. We present the framework and a real-world rollout dataset containing observations, generated programs, actions, and feedback to support research on how coding agents reason and act in the physical world.
[25.11.2026 06:26] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет ACAP — асинхронный фреймворк, в котором управление роботом строится как команда постоянных LLM-агентов, каждый из которых отвечает за свою группу звеньев, например манипулятор с захватом или торс и шасси. Для учёта физических зависимостей между компонентами вводится hyper-agent, который распределяет задачи, координирует взаимосвязи и динамически передаёт контроль между ведущим и вспомогательными агентами. По результатам 72 реальных запусков время выполнения задач сократилось на 28,2% по сравнению с одноагентной Direct Policy, а также подход показал преимущества над Fixed Delegation. Abstract  We propose loom attention, which allows a Transformer to dynamically rewire its circuits at every token by interweaving a head's vertical attention over the outputs of earlier heads (to obtain its query, key and value) with its horizontal attention over tokens. We instantiate vertical attention efficiently with normalized and gated linear attention over a per-token matrix memory, which stores the compressed head outputs and serves as a second residual stream. This yields LoomFormer, a dual-stream architecture that splits control and content between the vector and matrix streams. Across model sizes and training token budgets in language modeling and downstream tasks, LoomFormer matches Transformers trained with over 3x compute and recent residual-stream baselines (HC/mHC, MUDDFormer, AttnRes) trained with ~1.7-2.8x. For example, trained on the same 300B tokens with only 2% extra FLOPs, LoomPythia-2.8B achieves ~1.6x the average downstream gain of mHC-Pythia-2.8B over Pythia-2.8B, and matches (0-shot) or surpasses (5-shot) Pythia-12B. Code is available at https://github.com/Caiyun-AI/LoomFormer
[25.11.2026 06:26] Response: {"desc": "Авторы предлагают механизм loom attention, который позволяет Transformer динамически перестраивать свои внутренние связи на каждом токене. Вертикальное внимание над выходами предыдущих голов сочетается с горизонтальным вниманием по токенам, а вертикальная часть реализуется через нормализованный gated linear attention над матричной памятью для каждого токена. Так получается архитектура LoomFormer с двумя потоками: векторным, отвечающим за контроль, и матричным, хранящим содержимое. По результатам экспериментов в языковом моделировании и downstream-задачах LoomFormer сопоставим с Transformer, обученными на существенно большем объёме вычислений, и превосходит residual-stream baselines, например LoomPythia-2.8B почти в 1.6 раза опережает mHC-Pythia-2.8B по среднему приросту.", "emoji": "🧶", "title": "Динамическая перекройка внимания: контроль и содержимое в двух потоках"}

Abstract  Spatial intelligence, the ability to reason about 3D space from visual observations, becomes essential as multimodal large language models (MLLMs) move into the physical world. Beyond perceiving each view, it rests on two cornerstones of human cognition: fusing multiple views into one coherent 3D scene and mentally transforming that scene to new viewpoints as reasoning unfolds. Yet our spatial probes reveal that MLLMs largely lack both capabilities, even after spatial fine-tuning. We therefore present S-Mind, which internalizes multi-view geometry within the MLLM through a compact set of spatial tokens. It supervises these tokens to hold the fused 3D scene and re-emits them to re-express this scene from every viewpoint the reasoning adopts. Across four spatial benchmarks, S-Mind improves its Qwen3.5 backbones by up to 22.26 points and surpasses frontier general models such as GPT-6-Astra (+0.45), Kimi-K3.1-Pro (+1.97), and GLM-5.3-Flash (+7.80) in average accuracy. Moreover, the same probes confirm that it acquires both capabilities, reaching fusion and transformation accuracies of 65.11% and 82.17%, against at most 16.02% and 37.28% for the baselines. At inference, S-Mind also runs about 100x faster than a tool-based agent on the same backbone. Instilled through these spatial tokens during training, spatial capability becomes intrinsic to the backbone and persists even when the tokens are withheld, offering a promising path toward spatial intelligence in MLLMs.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Spatial intelligence, the ability to reason about 3D space from visual observations, becomes essential as multimodal large language models (MLLMs) move into the physical world. Beyond perceiving each view, it rests on two cornerstones of human cognition: fusing multiple views into one coherent 3D scene and mentally transforming that scene to new viewpoints as reasoning unfolds. Yet our spatial probes reveal that MLLMs largely lack both capabilities, even after spatial fine-tuning. We therefore present S-Mind, which internalizes multi-view geometry within the MLLM through a compact set of spatial tokens. It supervises these tokens to hold the fused 3D scene and re-emits them to re-express this scene from every viewpoint the reasoning adopts. Across four spatial benchmarks, S-Mind improves its Qwen3.5 backbones by up to 22.26 points and surpasses frontier general models such as GPT-6-Astra (+0.45), Kimi-K3.1-Pro (+1.97), and GLM-5.3-Flash (+7.80) in average accuracy. Moreover, the same probes confirm that it acquires both capabilities, reaching fusion and transformation accuracies of 65.11% and 82.17%, against at most 16.02% and 37.28% for the baselines. At inference, S-Mind also runs about 100x faster than a tool-based agent on the same backbone. Instilled through these spatial tokens during training, spatial capability becomes intrinsic to the backbone and persists even when the tokens are withheld, offering a promising path toward spatial intelligence in MLLMs."

Abstract A training-free agentic framework integrates pretrained 3D and 2D experts with closed-loop refinement to achieve state-of-the-art 3D part segmentation across multiple benchmarks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Segmenting a 3D object into semantically meaningful parts requires joint reasoning about geometric structure and semantic context. Pretrained 3D experts provide spatially grounded region proposals, while 2D experts provide semantic cues for identifying object parts. The challenge is to integrate these complementary capabilities into a framework that can both produce meaningful part segmentations and progressively refine them. We present PartAgent, a training-free agentic framework for 3D part segmentation built upon two complementary modules: (i) Structural-Semantic Expert Integration combines regions predicted by the 3D expert with multiview semantic evidence from the 2D expert through image-to-surface correspondences, constructing compositional part partitions at multiple granularities. (ii) Observation-Driven Closed-Loop Partition Refinement observes the current partition and its supporting evidence to select targeted inspection, evidence repair, composition, or reselection actions. It verifies proposed changes and uses the updated state and action outcomes to guide subsequent decisions. On PartObjaverse-Tiny-WT, PartAgent achieves 68.41 mIoU, outperforming the strongest competing method by 8.53 points. Across 3DCoMPaT200, PartNeXt, PartNetE, and PartObjaverse-Tiny, PartAgent achieves the highest mIoU on each dataset, averaging 69.36 across the four datasets, 7.28 points above the highest mean among competing methods.
[25.11.2026 06:26] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет PartAgent — агентную систему для сегментации 3D-объектов на части без дополнительного обучения (training-free). Модуль замкнутого цикла уточнения анализирует текущее разбиение и выбирает действия: проверку, исправление доказательств, композицию или переотбор, после чего проверяет изменения и учитывает их результаты. Abstract  We report the development of Antom 3-in-1 Transformer, a payment foundation model that integrates sequence, tabular, and graph representations for payment risk detection. The model contains approximately 7B parameters, with approximately 1.7B activated per task route. [25.11.2026 06:27] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Abstract  We present KMed-31B, a medical large language model post-trained from an open-weight model for on-premises deployment with limited hardware. Post-training a model for diverse medical tasks remains difficult, because improving one kind of task can lower performance on another. We attribute this to differences between tasks in expected output and available feedback. Medical question answering and diagnosis prediction have reference answers, whereas clinical inquiry has no single correct response and is judged with rubrics. We therefore divide medical training tasks into four categories by these two properties, train one specialist per category with a reward that its feedback supports, and integrate the specialists into a single student with Multi-teacher On-policy Delta Distillation (MOPD^2). For clinical inquiry, we build Synthetic Health Dialogue, a simulated corpus of consultations with lay users and clinicians whose patient cases span common clinical presentations in primary care. Relative to its base model, KMed-31B raises HealthBench Hard from 22.95 to 41.87 and HealthBench Professional from 37.92 to 50.20 in non-thinking mode, and from 23.80 to 51.48 and from 41.97 to 59.18 in thinking mode, while its scores on four multiple-choice medical benchmarks are maintained or slightly improved. KMed-31B has the highest unadjusted HealthBench Hard score among the compared models, including proprietary ones, and scores higher on HealthBench Professional than every open-weight model we compare, including models with more than ten times as many total parameters. Applied to another base model, Qwen3.8-27B, the same training recipe also raises both HealthBench scores. We describe the training recipe, data construction, and settings in detail so that institutions requiring on-premises deployment can develop medical models from open-weight foundations.

Знания специалистов объединяются в единого student через метод MOPD^2 (Multi-teacher On-policy Delta Distillation), а для задач клинического опроса создан синтетический корпус Synthetic Health Dialogue. Abstract PhyCoVE, a 533-case benchmark, and counterfactual fidelity evaluate video editing models on physical counterfactual edits involving rigid, elastic, and fluid dynamics, showing that even the best model reproduces only about one seventh of the physical change.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Physical counterfactual video editing turns a source video and a language-specified physical edit at a given execution frame into a counterfactual video that shows how the scene would evolve after the edit. Existing evaluations with counterfactual target videos mainly cover object removal or specific rigid-body motions, although real scenes also involve elastic deformation, fluid flow, and interactions among rigid bodies, elastic solids, and fluids. We introduce PhyCoVE, a benchmark of 533 cases on 110 source videos that covers rigid, elastic, and fluid dynamics in five interaction patterns, with edits that remove an object, change a physical quantity, or insert a new object. For the simulated cases, a language model proposes scene designs, coding agents build them with solvers suited to each material and physically based rendering and then simulate each edit from the source scene state, and humans check every stage. For the real cases, we record continuously from one camera position before and after each edit. We also propose counterfactual fidelity, a metric that applies across materials and their interactions and measures how much of the physical change from the source video to the target video a generated video reproduces. We evaluate seven open-source and commercial video editing models and find that counterfactual fidelity ranks them nearly as human preference does, but even the best model reproduces only about one seventh of the physical change. The models change how a scene looks but rarely how it evolves, whereas a physical edit requires understanding the dynamics that it changes. Physical counterfactual video editing thus remains far from solved, and PhyCoVE provides a foundation for developing and evaluating methods for it.
[25.11.2026 06:27] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет PhyCoVE — бенчмарк из 533 кейсов на 110 исходных видео для оценки моделей физического контрфактического редактирования видео, охватывающих твёрдые тела, эластичные материалы и жидкости. Данные созданы комбинированным способом: симуляции, построенные LLM и кодирующими агентами с проверкой людьми, и реальные записи с одной камеры до и после правки. Авторы вводят метрику counterfactual fidelity, которая измеряет долю физического изменения, воспроизведённую сгенерированным видео. Оценка семи моделей показала, что даже лучшая воспроизводит лишь около одной седьмой физического изменения, так как модели меняют внешний вид сцены, но плохо передают её динамику.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Видео-модели меняют внешний вид сцены, но не её физику"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract PhyCoVE, a 533-case benchmark, and counterfactual fidelity evaluate video editing models on physical counterfactual edits involving rigid, elastic, and fluid dynamics, showing that even the best model reproduces only about one seventh of the physical change.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Physical counterfactual video editing turns a source video and a language-specified physical edit at a given execution frame into a counterfactual video that shows how the scene would evolve after the edit. Existing evaluations with counterfactual target videos mainly cover object removal or specific rigid-body motions, although real scenes also involve elastic deformation, fluid flow, and interactions among rigid bodies, elastic solids, and fluids. We introduce PhyCoVE, a benchmark of 533 cases on 110 source videos that covers rigid, elastic, and fluid dynamics in five interaction patterns, with edits that remove an object, change a physical quantity, or insert a new object. For the simulated cases, a language model proposes scene designs, coding agents build them with solvers suited to each material and physically based rendering and then simulate each edit from the source scene state, and humans check every stage. For the real cases, we record continuously from one camera position before and after each edit. We also propose counterfactual fidelity, a metric that applies across materials and their interactions and measures how much of the physical change from the source video to the target video a generated video reproduces. We evaluate seven open-source and commercial video editing models and find that counterfactual fidelity ranks them nearly as human preference does, but even the best model reproduces only about one seventh of the physical change. The models change how a scene looks but rarely how it evolves, whereas a physical edit requires understanding the dynamics that it changes. Physical counterfactual video editing thus remains far from solved, and PhyCoVE provides a foundation for developing and evaluating methods for it."

Abstract CUA-SEE is a closed-loop evaluation environment that co-evolves tasks and executable verifiers to adaptively measure computer-use agents’ capabilities, boundaries, and progress reliability.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Computer-use agents (CUAs) are rapidly improving, yet their evaluation remains largely static. Existing benchmarks provide a strong foundation, but fixed difficulty ceilings, limited feature coverage, and coarse aggregate scores progressively weaken their discriminative and diagnostic value. We introduce CUA-SEE, a closed-loop evaluation environment that co-evolves tasks and executable verifiers from rollout evidence. It disentangles three objectives: benchmark evolution expands evaluation scope through cohort-calibrated difficulty and coverage growth; diagnostic evolution increases resolution through agent-conditional capability-boundary probes; and verifier evolution improves precision by translating changing requirements into state-grounded checkpoints with adversarial validation. Across multiple agents, applications, and rounds, CUA-SEE generates challenging, measurable tasks while preserving process-level evidence. Our results position adaptive evaluation as a scalable counterpart to self-evolving agents, continuously tracking what agents can do, where their boundaries lie, and how reliably progress is measured.

Abstract LeadVLA is a structured multimodal policy that separates route execution into explicit path, pace, and residual components, trained and evaluated with generated closed-loop data and a benchmark, achieving the highest success among tested robot policies and transferring to unseen real environments without fine-tuning.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Robot leading requires executing a route while continuously adapting to a designated follower. Existing robot-leading systems often mediate follower understanding and physical route execution through explicit states, planners, or modular interfaces; directly learning this coupling through end-to-end trajectory prediction can instead entangle route execution with follower response. We introduce LeadVLA, a structured multimodal policy that preserves the route as an explicit Path reference while learning a Pace for route progress and a Residual for local follower- and scene-conditioned adaptation. To make this problem trainable and measurable, we develop LeadInfra, a configurable Unreal Engine-based closed-loop data-generation infrastructure from which we instantiate an approximately 4M-sample training corpus, and LeadBench, a standardized benchmark with 108 fully held-out closed-loop episodes. LeadVLA achieves the highest task success (39.6%) among evaluated robot policies on LeadBench while maintaining strong route fidelity and follower responsiveness, and transfers to unseen real-world environments without real-world policy fine-tuning. Project Page: https://louiszengCN.github.io/LeadVLA_PAGE/
[25.11.2026 06:27] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет LeadVLA — структурированную мультимодальную политику (policy) для робота-проводника, который ведёт за собой следующего за ним человека или робота. Модель разделяет управление на три явные части: Path (маршрут), Pace (темп движения по маршруту) и Residual (локальная поправка с учётом поведения последователя и сцены). Для обучения и оценки авторы создали LeadInfra — конфигурируемую инфраструктуру генерации closed-loop данных на базе Unreal Engine (около 4M обучающих примеров) и бенчмарк LeadBench из 108 полностью отложенных эпизодов. Abstract  Generating 3D indoor scenes requires a representation that captures object semantics, instance identity, height structure, and orientation while respecting room geometry. Methods that model scenes as sequences of object vectors learn spatial constraints implicitly, leaving collisions and boundary violations to losses or post-processing; semantic-map methods make occupancy explicit, but collapse instance and directional structure. We introduce RoomTokens, a sliced structured-token representation in which each token stores semantic class, instance-color identity, and facing field. This representation allows a scene to be generated as a discrete grid while preserving cues for instance grouping, orientation estimation, and 3D extent prediction. To learn the RoomToken distribution, we propose MDIRT (Masked Diffusion over RoomTokens), a masked discrete diffusion model that denoises semantic/instance-color/facing channels jointly with a shared cross-slice backbone. Architectural tokens are clamped as explicit floor- plan constraints, so the model samples furniture layouts that respect room geometry while retaining instance and object-attribute structure. Experiments on 3D-FRONT show that RoomTokens with MDIRT improve layout fidelity and physical consistency over sequence/vector- and map-based methods, suggesting structured spatial tokens as a practical choice for discrete 3D scene synthesis.

Для генерации этих токенов авторы вводят MDIRT, маскированную дискретную диффузионную модель (masked discrete diffusion), которая совместно деноизирует все каналы через общий cross-slice backbone. Abstract  Multi-reference image generation requires a model to preserve the appearance of selected subjects while arranging them according to an instruction. Despite recent progress in reference-based generation and compositional control, reliably organizing selected subjects into the intended scene remains challenging. Although existing methods provide various forms of compositional guidance, the planned relationships are not always explicitly instantiated for inspection and refinement before image synthesis. To address this limitation, we introduce MAGPIE, which represents the intended composition as a simplified 3D scene defined by an executable Blender program. A coding agent links each scene instance to its reference, checks the geometry and rendered view against the instruction, and revises the program to correct discrepancies. The final proxy rendering serves as a visual plan for the image generator. Using MAGPIE-Instruct-3K, we train the generator to follow this plan while drawing each subject's appearance from its reference. MAGPIE improves both Qwen and FLUX on DreamOmni2 and SconeEval. With FLUX, it improves overall success on DreamOmni2 by 28.21 percentage points over the original backbone and achieves the highest overall score among the models evaluated on SconeEval.

Abstract  Modern visual generative models operate on tokens, and every visual asset they receive, whether an identity, a scribble, a depth map, or a motion video, is tokenized and appended to the input token sequence. Consequently, conditioning quickly dominates the computation cost. Yet most of these tokens carry redundant spatiotemporal information. Our key insight is that identifying what is genuinely new is exactly the problem that classical video codecs have long solved. Thus, we first introduce a codec-based token pruning method that discards redundant tokens. Then, we propose an on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) strategy to train a DiT adapter capable of learning the codec pruning pattern well without quality degradation. Together, they form On-Policy Codec Distillation (CodecOPSD). We scale CodecOPSD to 16 condition modalities on our own video diffusion model, where it prunes 66% of the condition tokens on average with almost no quality drop. Therefore, denoising becomes 2x faster, and the cache memory shrinks by up to 73% in KV cache mode. CodecOPSD also extends to other state-of-the-art pretrained image and video generative models, where its benefit grows further with more conditions, longer frames, and higher resolution.
[25.11.2026 06:27] Response: {"desc": "Современные генеративные модели для изображений и видео работают с токенами, и каждое условие (идентичность, эскиз, карта глубины, видео движения) добавляется в последовательность, из-за чего вычисления быстро растут. Авторы предлагают CodecOPSD: метод прунинга токенов на основе классического видеокодека, который отбрасывает избыточную пространственно-временную информацию и оставляет только действительно новое. Затем DiT-адаптер обучается через on-policy self-distillation (OPSD), чтобы воспроизводить этот паттерн прунинга без потери качества. В экспериментах с 16 модальностями условий удаляется в среднем 66% токенов, денойзинг ускоряется примерно в 2 раза, а KV-кэш уменьшается до 73%.", "emoji": "🎞️", "title": "Сжимаем условия как видеокодек: меньше токенов, та же качество генерации"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Modern visual generative models operate on tokens, and every visual asset they receive, whether an identity, a scribble, a depth map, or a motion video, is tokenized and appended to the input token sequence. Consequently, conditioning quickly dominates the computation cost. Yet most of these tokens carry redundant spatiotemporal information. Our key insight is that identifying what is genuinely new is exactly the problem that classical video codecs have long solved. Thus, we first introduce a codec-based token pruning method that discards redundant tokens. Then, we propose an on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) strategy to train a DiT adapter capable of learning the codec pruning pattern well without quality degradation. Together, they form On-Policy Codec Distillation (CodecOPSD). We scale CodecOPSD to 16 condition modalities on our own video diffusion model, where it prunes 66% of the condition tokens on average with almost no quality drop. Therefore, denoising becomes 2x faster, and the cache memory shrinks by up to 73% in KV cache mode. CodecOPSD also extends to other state-of-the-art pretrained image and video generative models, where its benefit grows further with more conditions, longer frames, and higher resolution."

Abstract NightWatch adds a lightweight streaming watcher to vision-language computer-use agents to keep perception synchronized with dynamic interfaces, reducing latency and wasted time while maintaining or improving task success.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Computer-use agents (CUAs) built on vision-language models are increasingly entrusted with long-horizon tasks in live desktop, web, and mobile environments, where the screen keeps changing on its own. However, current CUAs lack real-time situational awareness: they perceive the environment through discrete snapshots, and the substantial inference latency of VLMs, compounded by highly dynamic interfaces, opens a perception-action gap in which the observed state frequently becomes stale before the corresponding action is executed. To bridge this gap, we present NightWatch, a plug-and-play framework that endows any CUA with continuous, temporally grounded perception without changing the agent's model, prompt, or action space. NightWatch couples the VLM agent with NightWatch-0.8B, a lightweight System-1 model that performs streaming monitoring of the interface state and keeps the agent synchronized with the evolving environment at low computational cost and latency, thereby grounding each decision in the current state rather than an outdated snapshot and reducing both stale actions and idle waiting while preserving task success. Experiments on desktop and dynamic web environments (OSWorld-Verified and DynaCU-Bench) show that NightWatch, driven by a watcher of only 0.8B parameters, reduces non-reasoning wall-clock time by 33% and wasted time by 53% on OSWorld while keeping task success on par, raises success on DynaCU-Bench from 39.5% to 44.2% with the large VLM agent, and is more efficient than passive-watching baselines such as AOI and PUSV under the same screenshot-only input, as well as a System-1 decision model more than 30times its size. Overall, NightWatch demonstrates that the timing of perception is as critical as its content, marking a step toward agents that maintain continuous awareness of their environment rather than perceiving it turn by turn.
[25.11.2026 06:27] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет NightWatch — лёгкий плагин для vision-language агентов, которые управляют компьютером (computer-use agents), и который обеспечивает им непрерывное восприятие интерфейса. Основная проблема в том, что VLM работают с дискретными снимками экрана, и из-за высокой latency инференса действие часто выполняется над уже устаревшим состоянием. Модель NightWatch-0.8B, работающая как System-1 компонент, выполняет streaming-мониторинг интерфейса и синхронизирует агента с изменяющейся средой без изменения самой модели, промпта или пространства действий. В экспериментах на OSWorld-Verified и DynaCU-Bench фреймворк сокращает время простоя и стейл-действия, сохраняя или повышая success rate.", "emoji": "👁️", "title": "Важно не только что видеть, но и когда: агент, который не отстаёт от экрана"}

Abstract LoopDVR converts pretrained Transformer-based dense visual recognition models into parameter-efficient looped networks by selectively reusing Transformer blocks with adapters, reducing parameters while preserving accuracy.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash We propose LoopDVR, a framework for converting pretrained Transformer-based dense visual recognition models into parameter-efficient looped networks. Rather than reducing the network width or depth directly, LoopDVR reuses Transformer blocks across layers in both the ViT backbone and Transformer decoder, reducing the number of parameters while retaining the original computational structure. However, naively sharing parameters can disrupt the distinct transformations learned at different depths. To address this challenge, we identify suitable sharing groups based on inter-layer representation dissimilarity and keep the final backbone block independent for multi-scale feature construction. We then initialize each shared backbone block with the pretrained weights of its group's terminal layer to better preserve the learned representations. An independent Loop Adapter at each layer further helps retain depth-specific transformations within shared blocks. Subsequently, we distill visual representations into the looped backbone and integrate it with a looped decoder for task-specific training. Experiments demonstrate that LoopDVR achieves the best accuracy--parameter trade-offs across dense recognition tasks. Compared with the reference models, it reduces parameters by approximately 28% while maintaining competitive accuracy. Notably, LoopDet-S achieves 50.9 box AP with only 7.0M parameters, highlighting its effectiveness in compact models.
[25.11.2026 06:28] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен LoopDVR — метод превращения предобученных Transformer-моделей для плотного визуального распознавания (ViT и декодер) в параметрически эффективные looped-сети. Вместо уменьшения ширины или глубины сети блоки Transformer переиспользуются между слоями, что сокращает число параметров при сохранении исходной вычислительной структуры. Для сохранения различий между слоями выбираются группы для разделения весов по межслойной непохожести представлений, а в каждый слой добавляется независимый Loop Adapter, а затем проводится дистилляция в looped-бэкбон. Эксперименты показывают снижение числа параметров примерно на 28% при сохранении конкурентной точности, а LoopDet-S достигает 50.9 box AP всего с 7.0M параметрами.", "emoji": "🔁", "title": "Меньше параметров, та же точность: переиспользуем Transformer-блоки"}

However, naively sharing parameters can disrupt the distinct transformations learned at different depths. To address this challenge, we identify suitable sharing groups based on inter-layer representation dissimilarity and keep the final backbone block independent for multi-scale feature construction. We then initialize each shared backbone block with the pretrained weights of its group's terminal layer to better preserve the learned representations. An independent Loop Adapter at each layer further helps retain depth-specific transformations within shared blocks. Subsequently, we distill visual representations into the looped backbone and integrate it with a looped decoder for task-specific training. Experiments demonstrate that LoopDVR achieves the best accuracy--parameter trade-offs across dense recognition tasks. Compared with the reference models, it reduces parameters by approximately 28% while maintaining competitive accuracy. Notably, LoopDet-S achieves 50.9 box AP with only 7.0M parameters, highlighting its effectiveness in compact models."

Abstract  Forward diffusion reinforcement learning (RL)---enabling deterministic ODE sampling without trajectory backpropagation---has grown increasingly popular for efficiently aligning diffusion models. However, we reveal that existing methods essentially rely on signed reward-weighted regression (RWR), regressing the trainable policy velocity onto rollouts from a behavior distribution. Unbiased baseline subtraction implicitly assumes that the policy velocity equals the behavior marginal velocity---an idealized assumption violated under classifier-free guidance (CFG) rollouts, offline/off-policy updates, and imperfect online self-consistency. This behavior--policy mismatch breaks baseline invariance and injects baseline-induced gradient drift, destabilizing training. To resolve this issue, we propose Instantaneous Trajectory Distribution Matching (iTDM), taking the infinitesimal limit of Trajectory Distribution Matching toward a time-local reward-guided target via the posterior-score identity to yield a sample-level gradient anchored at the behavior velocity without learning an additional reward-tilted target score. Tracking this velocity with an online auxiliary denoiser guarantees exact baseline invariance at the auxiliary optimum, effectively suppressing baseline drift across both online and offline RL. Extensive experiments across compositional generation, text rendering, and preference alignment show that iTDM achieves state-of-the-art in-domain and out-of-domain performance across group-wise online/offline RL and pairwise offline RL. Notably, on the challenging GenEval2 benchmark, iTDM converges nearly 3\times faster than competing methods while scaling smoothly to 0.87 without collapse. Furthermore, iTDM scales effectively to the 5.6B FLUX.3 model, achieving better performance across both in-domain and unseen metrics. Code is available at https://github.com/Luo-Yihong/iTDM

Авторы показывают, что существующие методы фактически опираются на signed reward-weighted regression, и что допущение о совпадении скорости политики со скоростью поведенческого распределения нарушается при CFG, офлайн-обновлениях и неидеальной онлайн-самосогласованности. Это приводит к смещению градиента, вызванному baseline, и к нестабильности обучения. Предложенный метод iTDM, основанный на бесконечно малом пределе Trajectory Distribution Matching, гарантирует точную инвариантность к baseline и показывает состояние-of-the-art результаты на задачах генерации, рендеринга текста и выравнивания предпочтений.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Стабилизируем обучение диффузионных моделей, выравнивая скорость политики с поведением"}

Abstract Residual Guidance is a training-free method that counteracts context inertia in streaming video generation by adding a residual from denoising the previous chunk under the new prompt to early denoising steps, improving prompt switching without training or per-model tuning.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Real-time interactive video generation allows users to change the prompt during generation to steer the evolving video. However, existing streaming models, which generate videos chunk by chunk, often keep following the previous prompt after a switch. We trace this failure to context inertia, where the previous chunk acts as a strong prior that suppresses the new prompt. Furthermore, we find that this inertia persists even when the cache retains only the previous chunk, yet discarding the context breaks visual continuity, which limits approaches that edit the cache. Rather than editing the cache, we propose Residual Guidance (RG), a simple, training-free method that counteracts context inertia with a correction measured outside the cache. Specifically, RG denoises the re-noised previous chunk once under the new prompt without the cache and adds the resulting residual to the early denoising steps of the next chunk, where its content is largely decided and the new prompt is most suppressed. Across settings ranging from a single switch to long multi-switch generation, RG substantially improves prompt switching capability on existing streaming models, including those with dedicated switching mechanisms, while largely retaining visual quality. As it requires no training or per-model tuning and adds only one forward pass per chunk, RG can be easily integrated into existing real-time video generation pipelines.
[25.11.2026 06:28] Response: {"desc": "Статья описывает метод Residual Guidance (RG) для потоковой генерации видео по частям (chunk-by-chunk), который решает проблему инерции контекста: модель продолжает следовать старому промпту после его смены. RG не редактирует KV-кэш, а один раз денойзит пересэмплированный предыдущий chunk под новым промптом без кэша и добавляет полученный residual к ранним шагам денойзинга следующего chunk. Метод не требует обучения или подбора параметров под конкретную модель и добавляет лишь один forward pass на chunk. Эксперименты показывают заметное улучшение переключения промптов на существующих streaming-моделях при сохранении визуального качества.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Новый промпт важнее старого контекста: смена сцены без переобучения"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Residual Guidance is a training-free method that counteracts context inertia in streaming video generation by adding a residual from denoising the previous chunk under the new prompt to early denoising steps, improving prompt switching without training or per-model tuning.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Real-time interactive video generation allows users to change the prompt during generation to steer the evolving video. However, existing streaming models, which generate videos chunk by chunk, often keep following the previous prompt after a switch. We trace this failure to context inertia, where the previous chunk acts as a strong prior that suppresses the new prompt. Furthermore, we find that this inertia persists even when the cache retains only the previous chunk, yet discarding the context breaks visual continuity, which limits approaches that edit the cache. Rather than editing the cache, we propose Residual Guidance (RG), a simple, training-free method that counteracts context inertia with a correction measured outside the cache. Specifically, RG denoises the re-noised previous chunk once under the new prompt without the cache and adds the resulting residual to the early denoising steps of the next chunk, where its content is largely decided and the new prompt is most suppressed. Across settings ranging from a single switch to long multi-switch generation, RG substantially improves prompt switching capability on existing streaming models, including those with dedicated switching mechanisms, while largely retaining visual quality. As it requires no training or per-model tuning and adds only one forward pass per chunk, RG can be easily integrated into existing real-time video generation pipelines."

Abstract  Understanding a scene requires both geometry and semantics: geometry recovers its spatial structure, and semantics separates it into objects. Current models obtain the two from separate networks or separate passes. We present PINE, a one-step Omni Diffusion Transformer that predicts the point map, the surface normals, and the instance map of an image in one forward pass. PINE treats each map as a token stream, and a stream mask decides which streams interact. A camera-aware ray encoding positions every token by its camera ray rather than its pixel. Grounding attention adds the affinity of pretrained DINOv3 features to the attention logits. Trained on only 139K synthetic images for geometry, PINE achieves state-of-the-art point maps whether or not intrinsics are given and the best average rank on surface normals. It also exceeds SAM on large objects with less than 1 percent of its training images, and it predicts all three maps 5.7x faster than MoGe-3 with SAM.

Каждая карта обрабатывается как отдельный поток токенов, а маска потоков определяет, какие потоки взаимодействуют между собой. Камерно-зависимое кодирование лучей (camera-aware ray encoding) привязывает токены к лучам камеры, а grounding attention добавляет к логитам внимания сходство признаков предобученного DINOv3. Abstract  World action models (WAMs) couple generative video modeling with robotic action prediction but typically require large video and action transformers, limiting deployment efficiency. We present LoopedWAM, a compact WAM that recurrently applies a shared six-block core in each expert, executing 30 transformer layers while storing only 12 blocks per expert. To account for their asymmetric inference costs, the video and action experts can operate at different depths: visual features are computed once per query, whereas action computation repeats at every sampling step. We further propose elastic self-distillation, which trains a single elastic checkpoint, LoopedWAM-E, across action-depth and sampling-step budgets. With only 0.58B policy parameters and no robot pretraining, LoopedWAM achieves 96.1% success on LIBERO, 91.9% on RoboTwin 2.0, and 52.2% on RoboCasaGR1, while using 7.4times less peak GPU memory than Fast-WAM under matched inference measurements. On LIBERO, LoopedWAM-E achieves 96.0% success in 27.6 ms or 95.2% in 24.7 ms on an H100, providing multiple accuracy-latency operating points from a single checkpoint; with two sampling steps and four action loops, it stays within 0.5 points of LoopedWAM on all three benchmarks. Ablations demonstrate that weight-shared depth preserves competitive performance, while aggressive action-depth reduction requires explicit budget-aware training. On a real AgileX PiPER arm, LoopedWAM succeeds in 71.3% of 80 trials across four tasks.
[25.11.2026 06:28] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается LoopedWAM — компактная модель мира для роботов (world action model), которая объединяет генеративное видеомоделирование и предсказание действий. Архитектура рекуррентно применяет общий блок трансформера, что даёт 30 слоёв при хранении лишь 12 блоков на эксперт и 0.58B параметров без предобучения на роботах. Видео- и action-эксперты работают на разной глубине: визуальные признаки вычисляются один раз, а вычисления действий повторяются на каждом шаге сэмплирования, что экономит ресурсы. Метод elastic self-distillation позволяет обучить один чекпоинт, который управляет компромиссом между точностью и задержкой, достигая 96.1% на LIBERO и 7.4× меньшего пикового потребления GPU памяти по сравнению с Fast-WAM.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Компактный робот-мозг: один чекпоинт, много режимов скорости и точности"}

Abstract An end-to-end multimodal agent framework autonomously researches products and creates selling-point-driven storyboards to guide a frozen video generator, trained via supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning to produce effective advertising videos.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Recent advances in video generation have made automated advertising production increasingly feasible. However, an effective advertisement must faithfully represent the product and communicate its selling points through compelling visual storytelling, which requires more than visual synthesis: it calls for deeper product understanding and strategic decisions about how to present product value. Crucially, product research and creative design are closely intertwined, as the information gathered about a product determines which features can be faithfully demonstrated and how they should be presented. In this paper, we introduce AdProducer, the first end-to-end agentic framework for advertising video creation, transforming concise creative briefs into finished advertisements. Our multimodal agent autonomously researches products through web and image search and translates its findings into selling-point-driven storyboards that guide a frozen video generator. To achieve this, we develop a dedicated data construction pipeline that yields two training datasets, AdProducer-SFT and AdProducer-RL, and train the agent through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with joint optimization of storyboard- and video-level rewards. We further introduce AdCreation-Bench, comprising 305 advertising briefs across eight domains, with two complementary metrics: PG-Score for product correctness and creative execution, and Ad-Score for overall advertising effectiveness assessed by an independent judge. Experiments demonstrate that AdProducer improves the PG-Score of MiniMax H3 from 17.3 to 51.3 and generalizes to Veo 3.1 without additional training, improving its score from 28.0 to 35.9, with consistent gains in Ad-Score, VideoScore2, and human evaluations. Code, models, and data are released.

Агент самостоятельно исследует продукт через веб- и поиск по изображениям и на основе найденных сведений формирует storyboard, ориентированный на ключевые преимущества продукта, который управляет замороженным видеогенератором. Агент обучается в два этапа: supervised fine-tuning и reinforcement learning с совместной оптимизацией наград на уровне storyboard и итогового видео. Abstract  Games, especially modern AAA games, challenge language models to perceive complex scenes, control actions, and make decisions over long horizons. Existing game benchmarks primarily test language models as computer-use agents that query a language model throughout play, incurring repeated inference cost and, in real-time games, acting while the environment continues to evolve. We ask whether coding agents can learn from game interaction to develop standalone game-playing programs that run without language model inference. We thus introduce Coding4Games, a benchmark for evaluating whether coding agents can develop standalone programs that play modern games through visual observations and standard game inputs. Coding4Games has three defining features: 1) Diverse Modern Games, covering 15 games with diverse visual, strategic, and control demands; 2) Development-Test Separation, in which a coding agent uses a sandbox and the standard player interface to iteratively develop a program, after which the selected program is frozen and independently tested without language-model inference; and 3) Outcome and Process Evaluation, which measures not only game performance and reliability but also how agents use feedback, revise programs, and resolve problems during development. Across 15 games, the best program per game outscores the strongest GUI baseline on 13 and ties it on one. The human player outscores all agents on 10 games. We additionally examine the effects of development budgets. Coding4Games evaluates whether coding agents can turn their understanding of complex interactive environments into programs that work reliably on their own.

Abstract  Visual agents navigate whole-slide images through magnified observations associated with claimed locations. However, a visually plausible observation does not necessarily originate from its claimed location, particularly across magnifications and in regions with similar tissue structures. We formulate cross-magnification provenance verification (CMPV) to evaluate whether visual evidence supports a claimed spatial association, recover the source when possible, and abstain when evidence is insufficient. We introduce PyraFault, a benchmark of 3.06 million coordinate-supervised examples from 2,200 cases across five TCGA cohorts, covering within-context substitutions, absent-source faults, and geometric perturbations. We further propose ZoomTrace, a claim-independent verifier that estimates the observation's source before evaluating its agreement with the claimed location. It combines physical-scale alignment, visual retrieval, geometric refinement, and reliability estimation using spatially disjoint competing matches. On 503,320 test examples, ZoomTrace achieves 0.969 AUPRC and 0.987 AUROC, compared with 0.789 and 0.903 for Efficient LoFTR. Further evaluations reveal trade-offs in reliability estimation and limitations in vision-language models, cross-dataset retrieval, and calibration. Integrating ZoomTrace into visual agent tool paths reduces exposure to corrupted observations and silent failures, while downstream accuracy improvements depend on the agent and verification threshold.

Для этого предложен бенчмарк PyraFault с более чем 3 млн примеров с координатной разметкой, включающий подмены внутри контекста, отсутствующие источники и геометрические искажения. Метод ZoomTrace сначала оценивает истинный источник наблюдения, используя выравнивание масштаба, визуальный поиск и геометрическое уточнение, и только затем сравнивает его с заявленным местом, а надёжность оценивается на основе пространственно непересекающихся конкурирующих совпадений. На тестовой выборке ZoomTrace показывает AUPRC 0.969 и AUROC 0.987, заметно превосходя Efficient LoFTR, а также выявляет ограничения vision-language моделей и калибровки.", "emoji": "🔬", "title": "Проверяй, откуда кадр: надёжная верификация увеличения в гистологии"}

Abstract  We present VenusOCR2.0, a compact 1B-parameter vision-language recognizer designed for both high-accuracy and high-throughput document parsing across text, tables, and formulas. To continuously improve recognition quality without self-training collapse, we build a bounded recursive self-improvement (RSI) data engine where the model acts strictly as a weakness probe, while external multi-expert medoid consensus, VLM visual verification, and factorized synthesis supply reliable supervision across pretraining, curated SFT, and RLVR. VenusOCR2.0 couples a dynamic-resolution SigLIP2 encoder and 2x2 spatial token merging with a Qwen3-0.6B decoder, adopting compact atomic OTSL tokens to shorten table sequences and reduce structural errors. To further eliminate the autoregressive decoding bottleneck, we introduce PTPv2, a lightweight (sim67M) decoupled parallel draft module that combines frozen multi-layer verifier conditioning, a standalone learned mask, and block-causal all-position next-token prediction. On OmniDocBenchv1.6, VenusOCR2.0 achieves an overall score of 96.45 and a state-of-the-art 98.57 formula CDM, substantially outperforming massive general-purpose VLMs such as Gemini-3-Pro (92.91) and Qwen3-VL-235B (89.78). Deployed in vLLM on a single H20 GPU, PTPv2 delivers a 2.96x single-request speedup and up to 2.36x higher batched throughput while guaranteeing lossless, token-for-token equivalence with standard autoregressive decoding.

Для улучшения качества используется ограниченный рекурсивный self-improvement (RSI) пайплайн, где модель лишь выявляет слабые места, а разметку обеспечивают мультиэкспертный консенсус, VLM-верификация и синтез данных на этапах pretraining, SFT и RLVR. "Abstract  We present VenusOCR2.0, a compact 1B-parameter vision-language recognizer designed for both high-accuracy and high-throughput document parsing across text, tables, and formulas. To continuously improve recognition quality without self-training collapse, we build a bounded recursive self-improvement (RSI) data engine where the model acts strictly as a weakness probe, while external multi-expert medoid consensus, VLM visual verification, and factorized synthesis supply reliable supervision across pretraining, curated SFT, and RLVR. Abstract  Fine-tuning a generator on a complete high-resolution canvas requires long image sequences and substantial computation, yet local regions can still exhibit unnatural repetitive stripes when training resources are limited. We introduce Qwen-Image-HD, which adapts Qwen-Image 2.0 to high-resolution synthesis through local training and blockwise autoregressive generation. Each prediction covers a target tile at 1K scale, conditioned on high-resolution upper and left neighbors and a compressed historical thumbnail. These conditions provide adjacent fine-scale structures and an overview of the scene generated so far. To integrate these complementary cues, we map both forms of history and the target to a shared canvas coordinate system to express their spatial relationships and scale differences. On Qwen-Image-Bench at 4K, Qwen-Image-HD achieves stronger overall evaluation performance than direct 4K generation with FLUX-3-Image-Klein. Compared with full-image 4K supervised fine-tuning of the same backbone, it achieves better generation quality with lower training compute and higher inference efficiency, while exhibiting fewer unnatural repetitive stripes in original-resolution comparisons. We further introduce a Wavefront-AutoRegressive inference schedule that generates tiles on the same anti-diagonal concurrently across GPUs to reduce single-image latency.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Fine-tuning a generator on a complete high-resolution canvas requires long image sequences and substantial computation, yet local regions can still exhibit unnatural repetitive stripes when training resources are limited. We introduce Qwen-Image-HD, which adapts Qwen-Image 2.0 to high-resolution synthesis through local training and blockwise autoregressive generation. Each prediction covers a target tile at 1K scale, conditioned on high-resolution upper and left neighbors and a compressed historical thumbnail. These conditions provide adjacent fine-scale structures and an overview of the scene generated so far. To integrate these complementary cues, we map both forms of history and the target to a shared canvas coordinate system to express their spatial relationships and scale differences. On Qwen-Image-Bench at 4K, Qwen-Image-HD achieves stronger overall evaluation performance than direct 4K generation with FLUX-3-Image-Klein. Compared with full-image 4K supervised fine-tuning of the same backbone, it achieves better generation quality with lower training compute and higher inference efficiency, while exhibiting fewer unnatural repetitive stripes in original-resolution comparisons. We further introduce a Wavefront-AutoRegressive inference schedule that generates tiles on the same anti-diagonal concurrently across GPUs to reduce single-image latency."

Abstract In one-step generator training, aligning the paired reconstruction path rather than the generative path improves recall and sample quality while accelerating both training stages and lowering FID.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Representation alignment markedly accelerates diffusion training, but one-step generators raise a question that diffusion does not: which training path should receive the loss? A one-step generator sees two kinds of inputs. Corrupted real images come with their clean counterpart, which provides an instance-paired target. Samples generated from pure noise have no corresponding real image, yet this is where the final model operates. We study this question on Priming, which trains one-step generators from scratch by reconstructing corrupted images and then matching feature distributions with optimal transport. Aligning the generative path, the natural choice, collapses recall (0.45!to!0.15--0.25) in all 17 configurations we test, including five target constructions and loss weights down to 0.05. Aligning the paired reconstruction path with a frozen DINOv2 encoder instead helps in every setting we test. A short analysis explains this asymmetry: when the target carries no instance information about the input, the optimal aligned feature is constant within each class, so the loss pulls samples toward class prototypes. A control confirms this: aligning the paired path to a random same-class image instead of the input's own image removes most of the gain. Inside the generator, paired-path alignment creates an interface block through which post-training routes noise, and the image semantics carried through it predict sample quality across 16 models. The resulting recipe, \method, accelerates both training stages on ImageNet 256!times!256: it matches the 160-epoch baseline with 2times fewer priming epochs, surpasses its final quality with 4times fewer post-training updates, and lowers FID to 1.30 (B) and 1.155 (L) at the standard budget. A joint single-stage variant trains native one-step generators and reaches FID 1.22 with 115M parameters.
[25.11.2026 06:29] Response: {"desc": "В статье исследуется, какой путь обучения одношагового генератора стоит выравнивать с признаками внешнего энкодера. Авторы показывают, что выравнивание генеративного пути, то есть сгенерированных из шума образцов, резко снижает recall, тогда как выравнивание парного пути реконструкции с замороженным DINOv2 стабильно помогает. Причина в том, что без информации об экземпляре оптимальный признак становится константным внутри класса, и лосс стягивает образцы к прототипам классов. Предложенный рецепт ускоряет обе стадии обучения на ImageNet 256x256 и снижает FID до 1.30 и 1.155, а совместный одностадийный вариант достигает FID 1.22.", "emoji": "🎯", "title": "Выравнивайте парный путь реконструкции, а не генеративный, и обучение одношагового генератора станет быстрее и качественнее"}
